```
"""Enrichment and cluster-level validation helpers for triage stages."""

from __future__ import annotations

import re
from pathlib import Path

from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize

_PATH_RE = re.compile(r"(?:src|supabase)/[\w./-]+\.\w+")
_EXT_SWAPS = {".ts": ".tsx", ".tsx": ".ts", ".js": ".jsx", ".jsx": ".js"}
_VALID_EFFORTS = {"trivial", "small", "medium", "large"}


def _require_organize_stage_for_enrich(stages: dict) -> bool:
    """Gate: organize must be done before enrich."""
    if "organize" in stages:
        return True
    if "observe" not in stages:
        print(colorize("  Cannot enrich: observe stage not complete.", "red"))
        print(colorize('  Run: desloppify plan triage --stage observe --report "..."', "dim"))
        return False
    if "reflect" not in stages:
        print(colorize("  Cannot enrich: reflect stage not complete.", "red"))
        print(colorize('  Run: desloppify plan triage --stage reflect --report "..."', "dim"))
        return False
    print(colorize("  Cannot enrich: organize stage not complete.", "red"))
    print(colorize('  Run: desloppify plan triage --stage organize --report "..."', "dim"))
    return False


def _underspecified_steps(plan: dict) -> list[tuple[str, int, int]]:
    """Return (cluster_name, bare_count, total_count) for steps missing detail or issue_refs."""
    results: list[tuple[str, int, int]] = []
    for name, cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).items():
        if cluster.get("auto") or not cluster.get("issue_ids"):
            continue
        steps = cluster.get("action_steps") or []
        if not steps:
            continue
        bare = sum(
            1
            for step in steps
            if isinstance(step, dict) and (not step.get("detail") or not step.get("issue_refs"))
        )
        if bare > 0:
            results.append((name, bare, len(steps)))
    return results


def _steps_with_bad_paths(plan: dict, repo_root: Path) -> list[tuple[str, int, list[str]]]:
    """Return steps referencing file paths that don't exist on disk."""
    results: list[tuple[str, int, list[str]]] = []
    for name, cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).items():
        if cluster.get("auto") or not cluster.get("issue_ids"):
            continue
        for i, step in enumerate(cluster.get("action_steps") or []):
            if not isinstance(step, dict):
                continue
            detail = step.get("detail", "")
            if not detail:
                continue
            bad: list[str] = []
            for path_str in _PATH_RE.findall(detail):
                path = repo_root / path_str
                if path.exists():
                    continue
                alt_ext = _EXT_SWAPS.get(path.suffix)
                if alt_ext and path.with_suffix(alt_ext).exists():
                    continue
                bad.append(path_str)
            if bad:
                results.append((name, i + 1, bad))
    return results


def _steps_without_effort(plan: dict) -> list[tuple[str, int, int]]:
    """Return (cluster_name, missing_count, total) for steps without effort tags."""
    results: list[tuple[str, int, int]] = []
    for name, cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).items():
        if cluster.get("auto") or not cluster.get("issue_ids"):
            continue
        steps = cluster.get("action_steps") or []
        if not steps:
            continue
        missing = sum(
            1 for step in steps if isinstance(step, dict) and step.get("effort") not in _VALID_EFFORTS
        )
        if missing:
            results.append((name, missing, len(steps)))
    return results


def _cluster_file_overlaps(plan: dict) -> list[tuple[str, str, list[str]]]:
    """Return pairs of clusters with overlapping file references in step details."""
    cluster_files: dict[str, set[str]] = {}
    for name, cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).items():
        if cluster.get("auto") or not cluster.get("issue_ids"):
            continue
        paths: set[str] = set()
```

## CONCRETE ACTION PLANS FOR 5 CODE CLEANUP ITEMS

### ITEM 1: `lang_run_overrides_hidden_sentinel`

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/_framework/runtime.py`

**Problem:** `LangRunOverrides` uses a module-level sentinel object `_UNSET` to represent "not specified" tri-state for nullable fields. The sentinel is checked against using identity (`is _UNSET`), which is hidden/implicit.

**Concrete Solution: Use a TypedDict + Literal union for explicit tri-state modeling**

**Step-by-step changes:**

1. **Replace the sentinel pattern with Literal types:**
   - Line 19: Delete `_UNSET = object()`
   - Line 40-53: Change `LangRunOverrides` dataclass to use `NotRequired` (TypedDict style) or add an explicit `set` field

   **Recommended approach:** Convert `LangRunOverrides` from a dataclass to a TypedDict with `NotRequired` fields and add a `"__unset__"` literal to distinguish "not provided" from `None`.

   ```python
   # OLD (dataclass with sentinel):
   @dataclass
   class LangRunOverrides:
       zone_map: FileZoneMap | None = _UNSET
       
   # NEW (TypedDict with NotRequired):
   class LangRunOverridesDict(TypedDict, total=False):
       zone_map: FileZoneMap | None
       dep_graph: dict[str, dict[str, Any]] | None
       # ... etc
   
   # But since you need to check "was this field explicitly set?", better approach:
   # Use a helper class that wraps a dict and tracks which keys were set:
   
   @dataclass
   class LangRunOverrides:
       zone_map: FileZoneMap | None | Literal["__unset__"] = "__unset__"
       dep_graph: dict[str, dict[str, Any]] | None | Literal["__unset__"] = "__unset__"
       # ... etc (replace all _UNSET with "__unset__")
   ```

2. **Update `_coerce_lang_override()` (line 147-159):**
   - Change the sentinel check: `if value == "__unset__": continue`
   - This makes the tri-state explicit in the type annotations

3. **Update `_apply_lang_overrides()` (line 162-171):**
   - Line 166: Change `if value is _UNSET:` → `if value == "__unset__":`

4. **Update imports:**
   - Remove any `Literal` imports if not present
   - Add `Literal` to the type imports at the top of the file

**Why this works:** The sentinel becomes a named, typed constant (`"__unset__"` string literal), making it searchable and explicit. Type checkers understand `Literal["__unset__"]` better than a mystery `_UNSET` object.

### ITEM 9: `policy_load_masks_corruption`

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/policy/project.py`

**Problem:** `load_policy()` (lines 18-29) silently swallows errors. If the JSON file is corrupted or the directory is inaccessible, it returns `{"rules": []}` instead of signaling an error. Callers can't distinguish "file doesn't exist" from "file is corrupted."

**Concrete Solution: Raise `CommandError` on corruption; let missing file be silent**

**Step-by-step changes:**

1. **Add import at the top (after line 7):**
   ```python
   from desloppify.base.exception_sets import CommandError
   ```

2. **Rewrite `load_policy()` function (lines 18-29):**
   ```python
   def load_policy(path: Path | None = None) -> dict[str, Any]:
       p = path or _default_policy_path()
       if not p.exists():
           return {"rules": []}
       try:
           data = json.loads(p.read_text())
       except json.JSONDecodeError as e:
           raise CommandError(
               f"Project policy file is corrupted ({p}): {e}",
               exit_code=1,
           )
       except UnicodeDecodeError as e:
           raise CommandError(
               f"Project policy file has encoding errors ({p}): {e}",
               exit_code=1,
           )
       except OSError as e:
           raise CommandError(
               f"Cannot read project policy file ({p}): {e}",
               exit_code=1,
           )
       if not isinstance(data, dict):
           raise CommandError(
               f"Project policy must be a JSON object, got {type(data).__name__}",
               exit_code=1,
           )
       data.setdefault("rules", [])
       return data
   ```

3. **Update all callers:**
   Files that call `load_policy()`:
   - `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/policy_cmd.py` (lines 33, 40, 62): Wrap calls in try/except to catch `CommandError` if needed, OR let it propagate (preferred).
   - `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/runner/orchestrator_codex_sense.py`: Check if error handling is needed.
   - `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/external.py`: Wrap in try/except if user-facing.
   - `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/batch/orchestrator.py`: Wrap in try/except if user-facing.

4. **Specific caller updates:**

   **`policy_cmd.py` (lines 33, 40, 62):** No change needed if `CommandError` is acceptable. If you want graceful degradation:
   ```python
   # Example: _cmd_policy_add
   def _cmd_policy_add(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
       text = getattr(args, "rule_text", "")
       if not text or not text.strip():
           print(colorize("  Rule text is required.", "red"))
           return
       try:
           policy = load_policy()
       except CommandError as e:
           print(colorize(f"  {e.message}", "red"))
           return
       # ... rest of function
   ```

   **`orchestrator_codex_sense.py`:** Search for usage and decide if it's acceptable to let `CommandError` propagate (preferred for CLI errors).

**Why this works:**
- Missing files are OK (silent default)
- Corruption is caught early and reported with context
- Callers can decide whether to handle or propagate the error
- User sees a clear error message, not silent degradation

### ITEM 21: `mixed_command_entrypoint_layout`

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/registry.py`

**Current state (from grep above):**

**Flat modules (no package):**
- `config.py`
- `detect.py`
- `dev.py`
- `exclude.py`
- `suppress.py`
- `viz.py` (also `cmd_tree`, `cmd_viz`)
- `zone.py`

**Packages with cmd.py:**
- `autofix/cmd.py`
- `langs/cmd.py`
- `move/cmd.py`
- `next/cmd.py`
- `plan/cmd.py` (+ `policy_cmd.py`, `override_resolve_cmd.py`)
- `resolve/cmd.py`
- `review/cmd.py`
- `scan/cmd.py`
- `show/cmd.py`
- `status/cmd.py`
- `update_skill/cmd.py`

**Problem:** Mixed conventions. Some commands are flat modules (e.g., `config.py`), others are packages (e.g., `autofix/cmd.py`). This inconsistency makes it hard to know where to add new code.

**Concrete Solution: Standardize on package + cmd.py pattern**

**Step-by-step changes:**

**Option A (RECOMMENDED): Migrate flat modules to packages**

1. **For each flat module** (`config.py`, `detect.py`, `dev.py`, `exclude.py`, `suppress.py`, `viz.py`, `zone.py`):
   
   **Example: migrate `config.py` to `config/cmd.py`**
   
   a. Create directory:
      ```bash
      mkdir -p desloppify/app/commands/config
      ```
   
   b. Move the file:
      ```bash
      mv desloppify/app/commands/config.py desloppify/app/commands/config/cmd.py
      ```
   
   c. Create `config/__init__.py`:
      ```python
      """config command package."""
      from __future__ import annotations
      from desloppify.app.commands.config.cmd import cmd_config
      __all__ = ["cmd_config"]
      ```
   
   d. Update registry.py (line 14):
      ```python
      # OLD: from desloppify.app.commands.config import cmd_config
      # NEW: from desloppify.app.commands.config.cmd import cmd_config
      ```

2. **Repeat for all 7 flat modules:**
   - `detect` → `detect/cmd.py`
   - `dev` → `dev/cmd.py`
   - `exclude` → `exclude/cmd.py`
   - `suppress` → `suppress/cmd.py`
   - `viz` → `viz/cmd.py` (move `cmd_tree`, `cmd_viz`)
   - `zone` → `zone/cmd.py`

3. **Update registry.py imports** (lines 14-30):
   ```python
   from desloppify.app.commands.config.cmd import cmd_config
   from desloppify.app.commands.detect.cmd import cmd_detect
   from desloppify.app.commands.dev.cmd import cmd_dev
   from desloppify.app.commands.exclude.cmd import cmd_exclude
   from desloppify.app.commands.autofix.cmd import cmd_autofix
   from desloppify.app.commands.langs.cmd import cmd_langs
   from desloppify.app.commands.move.cmd import cmd_move
   from desloppify.app.commands.next.cmd import cmd_next
   from desloppify.app.commands.plan.cmd import cmd_plan
   from desloppify.app.commands.suppress.cmd import cmd_suppress
   from desloppify.app.commands.review.cmd import cmd_review
   from desloppify.app.commands.scan.cmd import cmd_scan
   from desloppify.app.commands.show.cmd import cmd_show
   from desloppify.app.commands.status.cmd import cmd_status
   from desloppify.app.commands.update_skill.cmd import cmd_update_skill
   from desloppify.app.commands.viz.cmd import cmd_tree, cmd_viz
   from desloppify.app.commands.zone.cmd import cmd_zone
   ```

4. **Check for any internal imports:**
   ```bash
   grep -r "from desloppify.app.commands.config import\|from desloppify.app.commands.detect import" /path/to/code
   ```
   Update any found to use the new package paths.

5. **Final pattern:**
   - ALL commands are now packages
   - ALL have a `cmd.py` file with the handler(s)
   - ALL have an `__init__.py` for re-exports (optional but recommended)
   - Registry imports all from `.cmd` submodules

**Result:** Consistent, discoverable structure. New developers know to add code to `<command>/cmd.py` or create helper modules in the package.

### **ITEM 26: batch_normalization_phase_blend**

**Current State:**
`core_normalize.py` (444 lines) normalizes a **single batch result payload** through multiple validation phases:

**Phase 1 (lines 97-98):** Config validation → `_normalize_abstraction_sub_axes()`
**Phase 2 (lines 100-165):** Dimension judgment validation → `_validate_dimension_judgment()`
**Phase 3 (lines 168-266):** Issues validation + normalization → `_normalize_issues()`
**Phase 4 (lines 269-276):** Compute required low-score dimensions → `_low_score_dimensions()`
**Phase 5 (lines 278-297):** Enforce low-score issue coverage → `_enforce_low_score_issues()`
**Phase 6 (lines 300-319):** Compute quality metrics → `_compute_batch_quality()`

**Orchestrator:** `normalize_batch_result()` (lines 322-440, 119 lines)
- Validates assessments loop (lines 354-394) — extracts scores, validates notes, builds dimension_notes
- Validates judgment loop (lines 396-416) — cross-references with assessments
- Calls `_normalize_issues()` with normalized state (lines 418-424)
- Enforces low-score issues (line 425)
- Computes quality (lines 427-432)
- Returns 5-tuple: `(assessments, issues, dimension_notes, dimension_judgment, quality)`

**Intermediate Types (from imports):**
- `ReviewIssuePayload` (from contracts_types)
- `BatchDimensionJudgmentPayload`, `BatchDimensionNotePayload`, `BatchIssuePayload`, `NormalizedBatchIssue`, `BatchQualityPayload` (from core_models)

**Pipeline Structure:**

```
Input: payload[dict] → 
  Phase 1: Extract & validate assessments (key→score, build notes) →
  Phase 2: Validate dimension_judgment (cross-ref with assessments) →
  Phase 3: Normalize issues (filter, deduplicate, enforce coverage) →
  Phase 4: Compute quality metrics →
Output: (assessments, issues, dimension_notes, judgment, quality)
```

**Action Plan:**

1. **Refactor `normalize_batch_result()` to be a pure orchestrator**
   - Extract the assessments validation loop (lines 354-394) into `_validate_assessments()` helper
     - Returns: `tuple[dict[str, float], dict[str, BatchDimensionNotePayload], float]`
   - Extract dimension_judgment validation loop (lines 396-416) into `_validate_judgment()` helper
     - Returns: `dict[str, BatchDimensionJudgmentPayload]`
   - Keep calls to existing helpers: `_normalize_issues()`, `_enforce_low_score_issues()`, `_compute_batch_quality()`

2. **Clarify return type with a dataclass**

     ```python
     class NormalizedBatchPayload:
         assessments: dict[str, float]
         issues: list[BatchIssuePayload]
         dimension_notes: dict[str, BatchDimensionNotePayload]
         dimension_judgment: dict[str, BatchDimensionJudgmentPayload]
         quality: BatchQualityPayload
     ```
   - Update `normalize_batch_result()` signature:
     ```python

         payload: dict[str, object],

         log_fn=lambda _msg: None,
     ) -> NormalizedBatchPayload:
     ```
   - Return: `NormalizedBatchPayload(...)`

3. **Update call site in `orchestrator.py` line 405-410**
   - Change:
     ```python

     assessments, issues, notes, judgment, quality = result
     ```

   - To:

### **ITEM 27: subjective_metadata_import_rewires_global_providers**

**Current State:**

- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/base/subjective_dimensions.py`:
  - Defines `configure_subjective_dimension_providers()` (lines 40-60) which mutates `PROVIDER_STATE`
  - This is called **at module import time** in `desloppify/intelligence/review/dimensions/metadata.py` (lines 54-58)
  - Sets up dimension loaders when that module is imported

- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/dimensions/metadata.py`:
  - Is a compatibility layer re-exporting from `desloppify.base.subjective_dimensions`
  - **At module load time** (lines 54-58), it calls:
    ```python
    configure_subjective_dimension_providers(
        available_languages_provider=_available_languages,
        load_dimensions_payload_provider=_load_dimensions_payload,
        load_dimensions_payload_for_lang_provider=_load_dimensions_payload_for_lang,
    )
    ```

- Importers of this module (found earlier):
  - `desloppify/app/commands/scan/workflow.py` (line 52: imports `resettable_default_dimensions`)
  - `desloppify/engine/_scoring/subjective/core.py` (line 9: imports `dimension_display_name`, `dimension_weight`)

**Problem:**
- When either of those files imports from `intelligence.review.dimensions.metadata`, **global provider state gets wired up as a side-effect**.
- This is implicit, order-dependent, and pollutes the module namespace with initialization logic.

**Solution:**
Move the bootstrap call to a **single canonical location** that is explicitly invoked by the CLI entry point, NOT by quiet imports.

**Action Plan:**

1. **Create `desloppify/base/subjective_dimensions_bootstrap.py`** (new file):
   ```python
   """Bootstrap entrypoint for subjective dimension providers.
   
   Call once at CLI startup to wire up dimension metadata loaders.
   """
   
   from __future__ import annotations
   
   from desloppify.base.subjective_dimensions import (
       configure_subjective_dimension_providers,
   )
   from desloppify.intelligence.review.dimensions.data import (
       load_dimensions as _default_load_dimensions,
   )
   from desloppify.intelligence.review.dimensions.data import (
       load_dimensions_for_lang as _default_load_dimensions_for_lang,
   )
   from desloppify.languages import available_langs as _available_langs
   
   
   def bootstrap_subjective_dimensions() -> None:
       """Configure subjective dimension providers at CLI startup."""
       def _available_languages() -> list[str]:
           try:
               return list(_available_langs())
           except (ImportError, ValueError, TypeError, RuntimeError):
               return []
   
       def _load_dimensions_payload():
           return _default_load_dimensions()
   
       def _load_dimensions_payload_for_lang(lang_name: str):
           return _default_load_dimensions_for_lang(lang_name)
   
       configure_subjective_dimension_providers(
           available_languages_provider=_available_languages,
           load_dimensions_payload_provider=_load_dimensions_payload,
           load_dimensions_payload_for_lang_provider=_load_dimensions_payload_for_lang,
       )
   
   
   __all__ = ["bootstrap_subjective_dimensions"]
   ```

2. **Remove the bootstrap call from `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/dimensions/metadata.py`:**
   - Delete lines 54-58 (the `configure_subjective_dimension_providers(...)` call)
   - Keep all the imports and re-exports
   - File becomes purely a compatibility shim (no side effects)

3. **Add bootstrap call to CLI entry point** (`desloppify/cli.py`):
   - At the **very start** of `main()` function, after argparse setup but before command dispatch:
     ```python
     from desloppify.base.subjective_dimensions_bootstrap import (
         bootstrap_subjective_dimensions,
     )
     
     def main():
         # ... setup ...
         bootstrap_subjective_dimensions()  # ← Wire up metadata providers once
         # ... command dispatch ...
     ```

4. **Document in code:**
   - Add docstring to `desloppify/intelligence/review/dimensions/metadata.py`:
     ```python
     """Compatibility layer for subjective-dimension metadata helpers.
     
     Historically this module maintained its own metadata-building logic. The
     canonical implementation now lives in ``desloppify.base.subjective_dimensions``.
     Keep this module as a stable import surface for existing callers.
     
     NOTE: Provider wiring (configure_subjective_dimension_providers) happens
     at CLI startup via bootstrap_subjective_dimensions() in base.subjective_dimensions_bootstrap.
     """
     ```

**Result:**
- No more implicit global state mutation on import
- Single canonical bootstrap call at CLI startup
- Easy to test (mock bootstrap or call separately)
- Clear dependency: CLI → bootstrap → dimension providers

```
1→"""Shared internals for building per-file review context payloads."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import re
     6→from collections import Counter
     7→from pathlib import Path
     8→
     9→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import StateModel
    10→from desloppify.intelligence.review._context.models import ReviewContext
    11→
    12→
    13→def build_review_context_inner(
    14→    files: list[str],
    15→    lang: object,
    16→    state: StateModel,
    17→    ctx: ReviewContext,
    18→    *,
    19→    read_file_text_fn,
    20→    abs_path_fn,
    21→    rel_fn,
    22→    importer_count_fn,
    23→    default_review_module_patterns_fn,
    24→    func_name_re,
    25→    class_name_re,
    26→    name_prefix_re,
    27→    error_patterns: dict[str, re.Pattern[str]],
    28→    gather_ai_debt_signals_fn,
    29→    gather_auth_context_fn,
    30→    classify_error_strategy_fn,
    31→) -> ReviewContext:
    32→    """Inner context builder (runs with file cache enabled)."""
    33→    file_contents: dict[str, str] = {}
    34→    for filepath in files:
    35→        content = read_file_text_fn(abs_path_fn(filepath))
    36→        if content is not None:
    37→            file_contents[filepath] = content
    38→
    39→    prefix_counter: Counter = Counter()
    40→    total_names = 0
    41→    for content in file_contents.values():
    42→        for name in func_name_re.findall(content) + class_name_re.findall(content):
    43→            total_names += 1
    44→            match = name_prefix_re.match(name)
    45→            if match:
    46→                prefix_counter[match.group(1)] += 1
    47→    ctx.naming_vocabulary = {
    48→        "prefixes": dict(prefix_counter.most_common(20)),
    49→        "total_names": total_names,
    50→    }
    51→
    52→    error_counts: Counter = Counter()
    53→    for content in file_contents.values():
    54→        for pattern_name, pattern in error_patterns.items():
    55→            if pattern.search(content):
    56→                error_counts[pattern_name] += 1
    57→    ctx.error_conventions = dict(error_counts)
    58→
    59→    dir_patterns: dict[str, Counter] = {}
    60→    module_pattern_fn = getattr(lang, "review_module_patterns_fn", None)
    61→    if not callable(module_pattern_fn):
    62→        module_pattern_fn = default_review_module_patterns_fn
    63→    for filepath, content in file_contents.items():
    64→        parts = Path(filepath).parts
    65→        if len(parts) < 2:
    66→            continue
    67→        dir_name = parts[-2] + "/"
    68→        counter = dir_patterns.setdefault(dir_name, Counter())
    69→        pattern_names = module_pattern_fn(content)
    70→        if not isinstance(pattern_names, list | tuple | set):
    71→            pattern_names = default_review_module_patterns_fn(content)
    72→        for pattern_name in pattern_names:
    73→            counter[pattern_name] += 1
    74→        if re.search(r"\bclass\s+\w+", content):
    75→            counter["class_based"] += 1
    76→    ctx.module_patterns = {
    77→        d: dict(c.most_common(3))
    78→        for d, c in dir_patterns.items()
    79→        if sum(c.values()) >= 3
    80→    }
    81→
    82→    if lang.dep_graph:
    83→        graph = lang.dep_graph
    84→        importer_counts = {}
    85→        for filepath, entry in graph.items():
    86→            count = importer_count_fn(entry)
    87→            if count > 0:
    88→                importer_counts[rel_fn(filepath)] = count
    89→        top = sorted(importer_counts.items(), key=lambda item: -item[1])[:20]
    90→        ctx.import_graph_summary = {"top_imported": dict(top)}
    91→
    92→    if lang.zone_map is not None:
    93→        ctx.zone_distribution = lang.zone_map.counts()
    94→
    95→    allowed_review_files = {
    96→        rel_fn(filepath)
    97→        for filepath in file_contents
    98→        if isinstance(filepath, str) and filepath
    99→    }
   100→    issues = state.get("issues", {})
   101→    by_file: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
   102→    for issue in issues.values():
   103→        if issue.get("status") != "open":
   104→            continue
   105→        issue_file_raw = issue.get("file", "")
   106→        if not isinstance(issue_file_raw, str) or not issue_file_raw:
   107→            continue
   108→        issue_file = rel_fn(issue_file_raw)
   109→        if issue_file not in allowed_review_files:
   110→            continue
   111→        by_file.setdefault(issue_file, []).append(
   112→            f"{issue['detector']}: {issue['summary'][:80]}"
   113→        )
   114→    ctx.existing_issues = by_file
   115→
   116→    total_files = len(file_contents)
   117→    total_loc = sum(len(content.splitlines()) for content in file_contents.values())
   118→    ctx.codebase_stats = {
   119→        "total_files": total_files,
   120→        "total_loc": total_loc,
   121→        "avg_file_loc": total_loc // total_files if total_files else 0,
   122→    }
   123→    _ = (
   124→        ctx.codebase_stats["total_files"],
   125→        ctx.codebase_stats["total_loc"],
   126→        ctx.codebase_stats["avg_file_loc"],
   127→    )
   128→
   129→    dir_functions: dict[str, Counter] = {}
   130→    for filepath, content in file_contents.items():
   131→        parts = Path(filepath).parts
   132→        if len(parts) < 2:
   133→            continue
   134→        dir_name = parts[-2] + "/"
   135→        counter = dir_functions.setdefault(dir_name, Counter())
   136→        for name in func_name_re.findall(content):
   137→            match = name_prefix_re.match(name)
   138→            if match:
   139→                counter[match.group(1)] += 1
   140→    ctx.sibling_conventions = {
   141→        d: dict(c.most_common(5))
   142→        for d, c in dir_functions.items()
   143→        if sum(c.values()) >= 3
   144→    }
   145→
   146→    ctx.ai_debt_signals = gather_ai_debt_signals_fn(file_contents, rel_fn=rel_fn)
   147→    ctx.auth_patterns = gather_auth_context_fn(file_contents, rel_fn=rel_fn)
   148→
   149→    strategies: dict[str, str] = {}
   150→    for filepath, content in file_contents.items():
   151→        strategy = classify_error_strategy_fn(content)
   152→        if strategy:
   153→            strategies[rel_fn(filepath)] = strategy
   154→    ctx.error_strategies = strategies
   155→
   156→    ctx.normalize_sections(strict=True)
   157→    return ctx
   158→
   159→
   160→__all__ = [
   161→    "build_review_context_inner",
   162→]
   163→
```

```
1→"""Payload assembly helpers for holistic review orchestration."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from typing import Any
     6→
     7→from desloppify.intelligence.review._prepare.issue_history import (
     8→    ReviewHistoryOptions,
     9→    build_batch_issue_focus,
    10→    build_issue_history_context,
    11→)
    12→
    13→from .prepare_holistic_scope import filter_batches_to_file_scope
    14→
    15→
    16→def _build_selected_prompts(
    17→    dims: list[str],
    18→    holistic_prompts: dict[str, Any],
    19→    per_file_prompts: dict[str, Any],
    20→) -> dict[str, dict[str, object]]:
    21→    """Build the dimension-to-prompt mapping, preferring holistic prompts."""
    22→    selected: dict[str, dict[str, object]] = {}
    23→    for dim in dims:
    24→        prompt = holistic_prompts.get(dim)
    25→        if prompt is None:
    26→            prompt = per_file_prompts.get(dim)
    27→        if prompt is None:
    28→            continue
    29→        selected[dim] = prompt
    30→    return selected
    31→
    32→
    33→def _attach_issue_history_context(
    34→    payload: dict[str, Any],
    35→    batches: list[dict[str, Any]],
    36→    state: dict,
    37→    options: object,
    38→    allowed_review_files: set[str],
    39→) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
    40→    """Attach issue history to payload and per-batch focus; re-scope batches."""
    41→    if not options.include_issue_history:
    42→        return batches
    43→    history_payload = build_issue_history_context(
    44→        state,
    45→        options=ReviewHistoryOptions(
    46→            max_issues=options.issue_history_max_issues,
    47→        ),
    48→    )
    49→    payload["historical_review_issues"] = history_payload
    50→    for batch in batches:
    51→        if not isinstance(batch, dict):
    52→            continue
    53→        batch_dims = batch.get("dimensions", [])
    54→        batch["historical_issue_focus"] = build_batch_issue_focus(
    55→            history_payload,
    56→            dimensions=batch_dims,
    57→            max_items=options.issue_history_max_batch_items,
    58→        )
    59→    return filter_batches_to_file_scope(
    60→        batches,
    61→        allowed_files=allowed_review_files,
    62→    )
    63→
    64→
    65→__all__ = ["_attach_issue_history_context", "_build_selected_prompts"]
    66→
```

```
1→"""File selection and staleness tracking for review."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import hashlib
     6→import logging
     7→import re
     8→from dataclasses import dataclass
     9→from datetime import UTC, datetime
    10→from pathlib import Path
    11→from typing import Any
    12→
    13→from desloppify.base.discovery.file_paths import rel
    14→
    15→from desloppify.base.discovery.source import read_file_text
    16→from desloppify.engine.policy.zones import (
    17→    REVIEW_SELECTION_EXCLUDED_ZONES,
    18→    zone_in,
    19→)
    20→from desloppify.intelligence.review.context import (
    21→    abs_path,
    22→    dep_graph_lookup,
    23→    importer_count,
    24→)
    25→from desloppify.intelligence.review.selection_cache import (
    26→    count_fresh,
    27→    count_stale,
    28→    get_file_issues,
    29→)
    30→from desloppify.languages import get_lang
    31→
    32→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    33→
    34→
    35→# Files with these name patterns have low subjective review value —
    36→# they're mostly declarations (types, constants, enums) not logic.
    37→LOW_VALUE_NAMES = re.compile(r"(?:^|/)(?:types|constants|enums|index)\.[a-z]+$")
    38→# Minimum LOC to be worth a review slot.
    39→MIN_REVIEW_LOC = 20
    40→
    41→
    42→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    43→class ReviewSelectionOptions:
    44→    """Configuration for review file selection."""
    45→
    46→    max_files: int | None = None
    47→    max_age_days: int = 30
    48→    force_refresh: bool = True
    49→    files: list[str] | None = None
    50→
    51→
    52→def hash_file(filepath: str) -> str:
    53→    """Compute a content hash for a file."""
    54→    try:
    55→        content = Path(filepath).read_bytes()
    56→        return hashlib.sha256(content).hexdigest()[:16]
    57→    except OSError:
    58→        return ""
    59→
    60→
    61→def select_files_for_review(
    62→    lang: Any,
    63→    path: Path,
    64→    state: dict,
    65→    options: ReviewSelectionOptions | None = None,
    66→) -> list[str]:
    67→    """Select production files for review, priority-sorted.
    68→
    69→    If *files* is provided, skip file_finder (avoids redundant filesystem walks).
    70→    """
    71→    resolved_options = options or ReviewSelectionOptions()
    72→
    73→    files = resolved_options.files
    74→    if files is None:
    75→        files = lang.file_finder(path) if lang.file_finder else []
    76→
    77→    cache = state.get("review_cache", {}).get("files", {})
    78→    now = datetime.now(UTC)
    79→    candidates = []
    80→
    81→    for filepath in files:
    82→        rpath = rel(filepath)
    83→
    84→        # Skip non-production files
    85→        if lang.zone_map is not None:
    86→            zone = lang.zone_map.get(filepath)
    87→            if zone_in(zone, REVIEW_SELECTION_EXCLUDED_ZONES):
    88→                continue
    89→
    90→        # Skip if cached, content unchanged, and not stale
    91→        if not resolved_options.force_refresh:
    92→            entry = cache.get(rpath)
    93→            if entry:
    94→                current_hash = hash_file(abs_path(filepath))
    95→                if current_hash and current_hash == entry.get("content_hash"):
    96→                    reviewed_at = entry.get("reviewed_at", "")
    97→                    if reviewed_at:
    98→                        try:
    99→                            reviewed = datetime.fromisoformat(reviewed_at)
   100→                            age_days = (now - reviewed).days
   101→                            if age_days <= resolved_options.max_age_days:
   102→                                continue  # Still fresh
   103→                        except (ValueError, TypeError) as exc:
   104→                            entry["reviewed_at"] = ""
   105→                            logger.debug(
   106→                                "Invalid reviewed_at value %r for %s: %s",
   107→                                reviewed_at,
   108→                                rpath,
   109→                                exc,
   110→                            )
   111→
   112→        priority = _compute_review_priority(filepath, lang, state)
   113→        if priority >= 0:  # Negative = filtered out (too small)
   114→            candidates.append((filepath, priority))
   115→
   116→    candidates.sort(key=lambda x: -x[1])
   117→    selected = [f for f, _ in candidates]
   118→    if resolved_options.max_files is None:
   119→        return selected
   120→    return selected[: resolved_options.max_files]
   121→
   122→
   123→def _compute_review_priority(filepath: str, lang, state: dict) -> int:
   124→    """Higher = more important to review.
   125→
   126→    Prioritizes implementation files with high blast radius and existing issues.
   127→    Deprioritizes types/constants files (low subjective review value).
   128→    """
   129→    score = 0
   130→    rpath = rel(filepath)
   131→
   132→    content = read_file_text(abs_path(filepath))
   133→    loc = len(content.splitlines()) if content is not None else 0
   134→
   135→    # Skip tiny files — not enough to review
   136→    if loc < MIN_REVIEW_LOC:
   137→        return -1
   138→
   139→    # Low-value files: language-provided pattern or generic fallback.
   140→    is_low_value = is_low_value_file(rpath, lang)
   141→
   142→    # High blast radius (many importers)
   143→    if lang.dep_graph:
   144→        entry = dep_graph_lookup(lang.dep_graph, filepath)
   145→        ic = importer_count(entry)
   146→        if is_low_value:
   147→            score += ic * 2
   148→        else:
   149→            score += ic * 10
   150→
   151→    # Already has programmatic issues (compound value — review will be richer)
   152→    issues = state.get("issues", {})
   153→    n_issues = sum(
   154→        1 for f in issues.values() if f.get("file") == rpath and f["status"] == "open"
   155→    )
   156→    score += n_issues * 5
   157→
   158→    # High-complexity files with wontfixed structural issues
   159→    # (mechanical detector says "complex" but can't say why — subjective review can)
   160→    n_wontfix_structural = sum(
   161→        1
   162→        for f in issues.values()
   163→        if f.get("file") == rpath
   164→        and f["status"] == "wontfix"
   165→        and f.get("detector") in ("structural", "smells")
   166→    )
   167→    if n_wontfix_structural:
   168→        score += n_wontfix_structural * 15  # Strong boost — these need human insight
   169→
   170→    # Complexity score from mechanical detectors (if available)
   171→    complexity_map = getattr(lang, "complexity_map", None)
   172→    if isinstance(complexity_map, dict) and complexity_map.get(rpath, 0) > 100:
   173→        score += 20  # Very complex files need subjective review most
   174→
   175→    # Larger files have more to review
   176→    score += loc // 50
   177→
   178→    # Low-value penalty — push toward bottom but don't exclude entirely
   179→    if is_low_value:
   180→        score = score // 3
   181→
   182→    return score
   183→
   184→
   185→def low_value_pattern(lang_or_name: Any = None) -> re.Pattern[str]:
   186→    """Return the low-value filename regex for a language, with generic fallback."""
   187→    if lang_or_name is not None and hasattr(lang_or_name, "review_low_value_pattern"):
   188→        pattern = getattr(lang_or_name, "review_low_value_pattern", None)
   189→        if isinstance(pattern, re.Pattern):
   190→            return pattern
   191→
   192→    if isinstance(lang_or_name, str):
   193→        try:
   194→            pattern = getattr(
   195→                get_lang(lang_or_name), "review_low_value_pattern", None
   196→            )
   197→            if isinstance(pattern, re.Pattern):
   198→                return pattern
   199→        except (ImportError, ValueError, TypeError, AttributeError) as exc:
   200→            pattern = None
   201→            logger.debug(
   202→                "Unable to load low-value review pattern for %s: %s", lang_or_name, exc
   203→            )
   204→
   205→    return LOW_VALUE_NAMES
   206→
   207→
   208→def is_low_value_file(filepath: str, lang_or_name=None) -> bool:
   209→    """Whether a file path is low-value for subjective review."""
   210→    pattern = low_value_pattern(lang_or_name)
   211→    return bool(pattern.search(filepath))
   212→
   213→
   214→__all__ = [
   215→    "LOW_VALUE_NAMES",
   216→    "MIN_REVIEW_LOC",
   217→    "ReviewSelectionOptions",
   218→    "count_fresh",
   219→    "count_stale",
   220→    "get_file_issues",
   221→    "hash_file",
   222→    "is_low_value_file",
   223→    "low_value_pattern",
   224→    "select_files_for_review",
   225→]
   226→
```

```
1→"""Historical review-issue context builders for retrospective review loops."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from collections import Counter
     6→from dataclasses import dataclass
     7→from datetime import datetime
     8→from typing import Any
     9→
    10→from desloppify.base.enums import issue_status_tokens, resolved_statuses
    11→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import StateModel
    12→
    13→_RESOLVED_STATUSES = resolved_statuses()
    14→_KNOWN_STATUSES = tuple(sorted(issue_status_tokens()))
    15→_AUTO_RESOLVE_NOTE = "not reported in latest holistic re-import"
    16→
    17→
    18→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    19→class ReviewHistoryOptions:
    20→    """Sizing controls for historical review-issue context payloads."""
    21→
    22→    max_issues: int = 30
    23→
    24→
    25→def _normalize_int(raw: object, *, default: int, minimum: int = 1) -> int:
    26→    try:
    27→        value = int(raw)
    28→    except (TypeError, ValueError):
    29→        return default
    30→    return value if value >= minimum else default
    31→
    32→
    33→def _trim(text: object, *, limit: int) -> str:
    34→    value = str(text or "").strip()
    35→    if len(value) <= limit:
    36→        return value
    37→    return value[: max(limit - 3, 0)].rstrip() + "..."
    38→
    39→
    40→def _timestamp_sort_key(value: object) -> tuple[int, str]:
    41→    raw = str(value or "").strip()
    42→    if not raw:
    43→        return (0, "")
    44→    try:
    45→        return (1, datetime.fromisoformat(raw.replace("Z", "+00:00")).isoformat())
    46→    except ValueError:
    47→        return (1, raw)
    48→
    49→
    50→def _issue_dimension(issue: dict[str, Any]) -> str:
    51→    detail = issue.get("detail")
    52→    if not isinstance(detail, dict):
    53→        return "unknown"
    54→    dimension = str(detail.get("dimension", "")).strip()
    55→    return dimension or "unknown"
    56→
    57→
    58→def _related_files(issue: dict[str, Any], *, limit: int = 6) -> list[str]:
    59→    detail = issue.get("detail")
    60→    if not isinstance(detail, dict):
    61→        return []
    62→    raw = detail.get("related_files")
    63→    if not isinstance(raw, list):
    64→        return []
    65→    out: list[str] = []
    66→    seen: set[str] = set()
    67→    for value in raw:
    68→        file_path = str(value or "").strip()
    69→        if not file_path or file_path in seen:
    70→            continue
    71→        seen.add(file_path)
    72→        out.append(file_path)
    73→        if len(out) >= limit:
    74→            break
    75→    return out
    76→
    77→
    78→def _iter_review_issues(state: StateModel) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
    79→    issues = state.get("issues")
    80→    if not isinstance(issues, dict):
    81→        return []
    82→    out: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
    83→    for raw in issues.values():
    84→        if not isinstance(raw, dict):
    85→            continue
    86→        if str(raw.get("detector", "")).strip() != "review":
    87→            continue
    88→        out.append(raw)
    89→    return out
    90→
    91→
    92→def _meaningful_note(issue: dict[str, Any]) -> str:
    93→    """Return the note if it's a real human-written note, not an auto-resolve boilerplate."""
    94→    raw = str(issue.get("note", "") or "").strip()
    95→    if not raw or raw == _AUTO_RESOLVE_NOTE:
    96→        return ""
    97→    return raw
    98→
    99→
   100→def _shape_issue(issue: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
   101→    """Shape a single issue into the payload format for the reviewer."""
   102→    detail = issue.get("detail") or {}
   103→    return {
   104→        "dimension": _issue_dimension(issue),
   105→        "status": str(issue.get("status", "open")).strip() or "open",
   106→        "summary": _trim(issue.get("summary", ""), limit=200),
   107→        "suggestion": _trim(detail.get("suggestion", ""), limit=200),
   108→        "related_files": _related_files(issue),
   109→        "note": _meaningful_note(issue),
   110→        "confidence": str(issue.get("confidence", "")).strip(),
   111→        "first_seen": str(issue.get("first_seen", "")).strip(),
   112→        "last_seen": str(issue.get("last_seen", "")).strip(),
   113→    }
   114→
   115→
   116→def build_issue_history_context(
   117→    state: StateModel,
   118→    *,
   119→    options: ReviewHistoryOptions | None = None,
   120→) -> dict[str, Any]:
   121→    """Build flat issue-history context for retrospective subjective review.
   122→
   123→    Returns the most recent review issues as a flat list, each with its
   124→    status, summary, suggestion, related files, and any human-written note.
   125→    """
   126→    resolved_options = options or ReviewHistoryOptions()
   127→    max_issues = _normalize_int(resolved_options.max_issues, default=30)
   128→
   129→    review_issues = _iter_review_issues(state)
   130→    if not review_issues:
   131→        return {
   132→            "summary": {
   133→                "total_review_issues": 0,
   134→                "open_review_issues": 0,
   135→                "status_counts": {status: 0 for status in _KNOWN_STATUSES},
   136→                "dimension_open_counts": {},
   137→            },
   138→            "recent_issues": [],
   139→        }
   140→
   141→    status_counts: Counter[str] = Counter()
   142→    dimension_open_counts: Counter[str] = Counter()
   143→
   144→    for issue in review_issues:
   145→        status = str(issue.get("status", "open")).strip() or "open"
   146→        status_counts[status] += 1
   147→        if status == "open":
   148→            dimension_open_counts[_issue_dimension(issue)] += 1
   149→
   150→    # Sort by last_seen descending, take the most recent N.
   151→    sorted_issues = sorted(
   152→        review_issues,
   153→        key=lambda f: _timestamp_sort_key(f.get("last_seen")),
   154→        reverse=True,
   155→    )
   156→
   157→    recent_issues = [_shape_issue(f) for f in sorted_issues[:max_issues]]
   158→
   159→    return {
   160→        "summary": {
   161→            "total_review_issues": len(review_issues),
   162→            "open_review_issues": int(status_counts.get("open", 0)),
   163→            "status_counts": {
   164→                status: int(status_counts.get(status, 0)) for status in _KNOWN_STATUSES
   165→            },
   166→            "dimension_open_counts": {
   167→                dim: count
   168→                for dim, count in sorted(
   169→                    dimension_open_counts.items(),
   170→                    key=lambda item: (-item[1], item[0]),
   171→                )
   172→            },
   173→        },
   174→        "recent_issues": recent_issues,
   175→    }
   176→
   177→
   178→def _normalize_dimensions(dimensions: object) -> list[str]:
   179→    if not isinstance(dimensions, list | tuple | set):
   180→        return []
   181→    out: list[str] = []
   182→    seen: set[str] = set()
   183→    for value in dimensions:
   184→        dim = str(value or "").strip()
   185→        if not dim or dim in seen:
   186→            continue
   187→        seen.add(dim)
   188→        out.append(dim)
   189→    return out
   190→
   191→
   192→def build_batch_issue_focus(
   193→    history: dict[str, Any],
   194→    *,
   195→    dimensions: object,
   196→    max_items: int = 20,
   197→) -> dict[str, Any]:
   198→    """Build a dimension-scoped issue slice for one review batch.
   199→
   200→    Filters the flat recent_issues list to only those matching the batch
   201→    dimensions, capped at max_items.
   202→    """
   203→    dim_list = _normalize_dimensions(dimensions)
   204→    dim_set = set(dim_list)
   205→    limit = _normalize_int(max_items, default=20, minimum=1)
   206→
   207→    all_issues = history.get("recent_issues", [])
   208→    if not isinstance(all_issues, list):
   209→        all_issues = []
   210→
   211→    filtered: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
   212→    for issue in all_issues:
   213→        if not isinstance(issue, dict):
   214→            continue
   215→        dim = str(issue.get("dimension", "")).strip()
   216→        if dim and dim in dim_set:
   217→            filtered.append(issue)
   218→            if len(filtered) >= limit:
   219→                break
   220→
   221→    return {
   222→        "dimensions": dim_list,
   223→        "max_items": limit,
   224→        "selected_count": len(filtered),
   225→        "issues": filtered,
   226→    }
   227→
   228→
   229→__all__ = [
   230→    "ReviewHistoryOptions",
   231→    "build_issue_history_context",
   232→    "build_batch_issue_focus",
   233→]
   234→
```

```
1→"""Orchestration helpers for holistic review payload preparation."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from dataclasses import dataclass
     6→from pathlib import Path
     7→from typing import Any
     8→
     9→from desloppify.intelligence.review._context.models import HolisticContext
    10→from desloppify.intelligence.review._prepare.helpers import HOLISTIC_WORKFLOW
    11→
    12→from .prepare_holistic_payload_parts import (
    13→    _attach_issue_history_context,
    14→    _build_selected_prompts,
    15→)
    16→from .prepare_holistic_scope import (
    17→    collect_allowed_review_files,
    18→    file_in_allowed_scope,
    19→    filter_batches_to_file_scope,
    20→)
    21→
    22→
    23→def _resolve_review_files(
    24→    path: Path,
    25→    lang: object,
    26→    options: object,
    27→) -> tuple[list[str], set[str]]:
    28→    """Resolve scoped review files and the allowed-review-file set."""
    29→    discovered_files = (
    30→        options.files
    31→        if options.files is not None
    32→        else (lang.file_finder(path) if lang.file_finder else [])
    33→    )
    34→    allowed = collect_allowed_review_files(discovered_files, lang, base_path=path)
    35→    scoped_files = [
    36→        filepath
    37→        for filepath in discovered_files
    38→        if file_in_allowed_scope(filepath, allowed)
    39→    ]
    40→    return scoped_files, allowed
    41→
    42→
    43→def _build_review_contexts(
    44→    path: Path,
    45→    lang: object,
    46→    state: dict,
    47→    review_files: list[str],
    48→    *,
    49→    is_file_cache_enabled_fn,
    50→    enable_file_cache_fn,
    51→    disable_file_cache_fn,
    52→    build_holistic_context_fn,
    53→    build_review_context_fn,
    54→) -> tuple[HolisticContext, object]:
    55→    """Build holistic and review contexts, managing the file cache lifecycle."""
    56→    already_cached = is_file_cache_enabled_fn()
    57→    if not already_cached:
    58→        enable_file_cache_fn()
    59→    try:
    60→        context = HolisticContext.from_raw(
    61→            build_holistic_context_fn(path, lang, state, files=review_files)
    62→        )
    63→        review_ctx = build_review_context_fn(path, lang, state, files=review_files)
    64→    finally:
    65→        if not already_cached:
    66→            disable_file_cache_fn()
    67→    return context, review_ctx
    68→
    69→
    70→@dataclass
    71→class _DimensionContext:
    72→    """Resolved dimension configuration for holistic review."""
    73→
    74→    dims: list[str]
    75→    holistic_prompts: dict[str, Any]
    76→    per_file_prompts: dict[str, Any]
    77→    system_prompt: str
    78→    lang_guide: str
    79→    invalid_requested: list[str]
    80→    invalid_default: list[str]
    81→
    82→
    83→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    84→class HolisticPrepareDependencies:
    85→    """Injected collaborators used by holistic payload orchestration."""
    86→
    87→    is_file_cache_enabled_fn: object
    88→    enable_file_cache_fn: object
    89→    disable_file_cache_fn: object
    90→    build_holistic_context_fn: object
    91→    build_review_context_fn: object
    92→    load_dimensions_for_lang_fn: object
    93→    resolve_dimensions_fn: object
    94→    get_lang_guidance_fn: object
    95→    build_investigation_batches_fn: object
    96→    batch_concerns_fn: object
    97→    filter_batches_to_dimensions_fn: object
    98→    append_full_sweep_batch_fn: object
    99→    serialize_context_fn: object
   100→    log_best_effort_failure_fn: object
   101→    logger: object
   102→
   103→
   104→def _resolve_dimension_context(
   105→    lang_name: str,
   106→    options: object,
   107→    *,
   108→    load_dimensions_for_lang_fn,
   109→    resolve_dimensions_fn,
   110→    get_lang_guidance_fn,
   111→) -> _DimensionContext:
   112→    """Load, resolve, and validate dimensions for the review."""
   113→    default_dims, holistic_prompts, system_prompt = load_dimensions_for_lang_fn(lang_name)
   114→    _, per_file_prompts, _ = load_dimensions_for_lang_fn(lang_name)
   115→    dims = resolve_dimensions_fn(
   116→        cli_dimensions=options.dimensions,
   117→        default_dimensions=default_dims,
   118→    )
   119→    lang_guide = get_lang_guidance_fn(lang_name)
   120→    valid_dims = set(holistic_prompts) | set(per_file_prompts)
   121→    invalid_requested = [
   122→        dim for dim in (options.dimensions or []) if dim not in valid_dims
   123→    ]
   124→    invalid_default = [dim for dim in default_dims if dim not in valid_dims]
   125→    return _DimensionContext(
   126→        dims=dims,
   127→        holistic_prompts=holistic_prompts,
   128→        per_file_prompts=per_file_prompts,
   129→        system_prompt=system_prompt,
   130→        lang_guide=lang_guide,
   131→        invalid_requested=invalid_requested,
   132→        invalid_default=invalid_default,
   133→    )
   134→
   135→
   136→def _append_concerns_batch(
   137→    batches: list[dict[str, Any]],
   138→    state: dict,
   139→    dims: list[str],
   140→    allowed_review_files: set[str],
   141→    max_files_per_batch: int,
   142→    *,
   143→    batch_concerns_fn,
   144→    log_best_effort_failure_fn,
   145→    log: object,
   146→) -> None:
   147→    """Generate concern signals and append as a batch (best-effort)."""
   148→    try:
   149→        from desloppify.engine._concerns.generators import generate_concerns
   150→
   151→        concerns = generate_concerns(state)
   152→        concerns = [
   153→            concern
   154→            for concern in concerns
   155→            if file_in_allowed_scope(getattr(concern, "file", ""), allowed_review_files)
   156→        ]
   157→        concerns_batch = batch_concerns_fn(
   158→            concerns,
   159→            max_files=max_files_per_batch,
   160→            active_dimensions=dims,
   161→        )
   162→        if concerns_batch:
   163→            concern_dim = concerns_batch["dimensions"][0]
   164→            merged = False
   165→            for existing in batches:
   166→                if existing.get("dimensions") == [concern_dim]:
   167→                    existing_files = set(existing.get("files_to_read", []))
   168→                    for filepath in concerns_batch.get("files_to_read", []):
   169→                        if filepath not in existing_files:
   170→                            existing["files_to_read"].append(filepath)
   171→                            existing_files.add(filepath)
   172→                    existing["concern_signals"] = concerns_batch.get("concern_signals", [])
   173→                    existing["concern_signal_count"] = concerns_batch.get("concern_signal_count", 0)
   174→                    merged = True
   175→                    break
   176→            if not merged:
   177→                batches.append(concerns_batch)
   178→    except (ImportError, AttributeError, TypeError, ValueError) as exc:
   179→        log_best_effort_failure_fn(log, "generate review concern batch", exc)
   180→
   181→
   182→def prepare_holistic_review_payload(
   183→    path: Path,
   184→    lang: object,
   185→    state: dict,
   186→    options,
   187→    *,
   188→    deps: HolisticPrepareDependencies,
   189→) -> dict[str, object]:
   190→    """Prepare holistic review payload with injected dependencies for patchability."""
   191→    scoped_files, allowed_review_files = _resolve_review_files(path, lang, options)
   192→
   193→    context, review_ctx = _build_review_contexts(
   194→        path,
   195→        lang,
   196→        state,
   197→        scoped_files,
   198→        is_file_cache_enabled_fn=deps.is_file_cache_enabled_fn,
   199→        enable_file_cache_fn=deps.enable_file_cache_fn,
   200→        disable_file_cache_fn=deps.disable_file_cache_fn,
   201→        build_holistic_context_fn=deps.build_holistic_context_fn,
   202→        build_review_context_fn=deps.build_review_context_fn,
   203→    )
   204→
   205→    dim_ctx = _resolve_dimension_context(
   206→        lang.name,
   207→        options,
   208→        load_dimensions_for_lang_fn=deps.load_dimensions_for_lang_fn,
   209→        resolve_dimensions_fn=deps.resolve_dimensions_fn,
   210→        get_lang_guidance_fn=deps.get_lang_guidance_fn,
   211→    )
   212→
   213→    batches = deps.build_investigation_batches_fn(
   214→        context,
   215→        lang,
   216→        repo_root=path,
   217→        max_files_per_batch=options.max_files_per_batch,
   218→    )
   219→
   220→    _append_concerns_batch(
   221→        batches,
   222→        state,
   223→        dim_ctx.dims,
   224→        allowed_review_files,
   225→        options.max_files_per_batch,
   226→        batch_concerns_fn=deps.batch_concerns_fn,
   227→        log_best_effort_failure_fn=deps.log_best_effort_failure_fn,
   228→        log=deps.logger,
   229→    )
   230→
   231→    batches = deps.filter_batches_to_dimensions_fn(
   232→        batches,
   233→        dim_ctx.dims,
   234→        fallback_max_files=options.max_files_per_batch,
   235→    )
   236→    include_full_sweep = bool(options.include_full_sweep)
   237→    if options.dimensions:
   238→        include_full_sweep = False
   239→    if include_full_sweep:
   240→        deps.append_full_sweep_batch_fn(
   241→            batches=batches,
   242→            dims=dim_ctx.dims,
   243→            all_files=scoped_files,
   244→            lang=lang,
   245→            max_files=options.max_files_per_batch,
   246→        )
   247→    batches = filter_batches_to_file_scope(
   248→        batches,
   249→        allowed_files=allowed_review_files,
   250→    )
   251→
   252→    selected_prompts = _build_selected_prompts(
   253→        dim_ctx.dims,
   254→        dim_ctx.holistic_prompts,
   255→        dim_ctx.per_file_prompts,
   256→    )
   257→
   258→    payload: dict[str, Any] = {
   259→        "command": "review",
   260→        "mode": "holistic",
   261→        "language": lang.name,
   262→        "dimensions": dim_ctx.dims,
   263→        "dimension_prompts": selected_prompts,
   264→        "lang_guidance": dim_ctx.lang_guide,
   265→        "holistic_context": context.to_dict(),
   266→        "review_context": deps.serialize_context_fn(review_ctx),
   267→        "system_prompt": dim_ctx.system_prompt,
   268→        "total_files": context.codebase_stats.get("total_files", 0),
   269→        "workflow": HOLISTIC_WORKFLOW,
   270→        "invalid_dimensions": {
   271→            "requested": dim_ctx.invalid_requested,
   272→            "default": dim_ctx.invalid_default,
   273→        },
   274→    }
   275→
   276→    batches = _attach_issue_history_context(
   277→        payload,
   278→        batches,
   279→        state,
   280→        options,
   281→        allowed_review_files,
   282→    )
   283→
   284→    payload["investigation_batches"] = batches
   285→    return payload
   286→
   287→
   288→__all__ = ["HolisticPrepareDependencies", "prepare_holistic_review_payload"]
   289→
```

```
    49→        f'  "batch_index": {batch_index + 1},\n'

    51→        '  "dimension_notes": {\n'
    52→        '    "<dimension>": {\n'

    89→def _extract_dimension_prompts(batch: dict[str, object]) -> dict[str, dict[str, object]]:
    90→    """Extract dimension prompts embedded by explode_to_single_dimension."""
    91→    prompt = batch.get("_dimension_prompt")
    92→    if not isinstance(prompt, dict):
    93→        return {}
    94→    dims = batch.get("dimensions", [])
    95→    if isinstance(dims, list) and len(dims) == 1:
    96→        return {str(dims[0]): prompt}
    97→    return {}
    98→
    99→
   100→def render_batch_prompt(
   101→    *,
   102→    repo_root: Path,
   103→    packet_path: Path,
   104→    batch_index: int,
   105→    batch: dict[str, object],
   106→    policy_block: str = "",
   107→) -> str:
   108→    """Render one subagent prompt for a holistic investigation batch."""
   109→    context = build_batch_context(batch, batch_index)
   110→    dim_prompts = _extract_dimension_prompts(batch)
   111→    return join_non_empty_sections(
   112→        _render_metadata_block(
   113→            repo_root=repo_root,
   114→            packet_path=packet_path,
   115→            batch_index=batch_index,
   116→            context=context,
   117→        ),
   118→        render_dimension_prompts_block(context.dimensions, dim_prompts),
   119→        policy_block,
   120→        render_scoring_frame(),
   121→        render_scan_evidence_note(),
   122→        render_seed_files_block(context),
   123→        render_historical_focus(batch),
   124→        render_mechanical_concern_signals(batch),
   125→        render_task_requirements(issues_cap=context.issues_cap, dim_set=context.dimension_set),
   126→        render_scope_enums(),
   127→        _render_output_schema(context, batch_index),
   128→    )
   129→
   130→
   131→__all__ = ["render_batch_prompt"]
```

```
    33→def coerce_string_list(raw: object) -> tuple[str, ...]:

    95→def render_historical_focus(batch: dict[str, object]) -> str:
    96→    focus = batch.get("historical_issue_focus")
    97→    if not isinstance(focus, dict):
    98→        return ""
    99→
   100→    selected_raw = focus.get("selected_count", 0)
   101→    try:
   102→        selected_count = max(0, int(selected_raw))
   103→    except (TypeError, ValueError):
   104→        selected_count = 0
   105→
   106→    issues = focus.get("issues", [])
   107→    if not isinstance(issues, list):
   108→        issues = []
   109→
   110→    if selected_count <= 0 or not issues:
   111→        return ""
   112→
   113→    lines: list[str] = []
   114→    lines.append(
   115→        "Previously flagged issues — navigation aid, not scoring evidence:"
   116→    )
   117→    lines.append(
   118→        "Check whether each issue still exists in the current code. Do not re-report"
   119→        " issues that have been fixed or marked wontfix — focus on what remains or"
   120→        " what is new. If several past issues share a root cause, call that out."
   121→    )
   122→
   123→    for entry in issues:
   124→        if not isinstance(entry, dict):
   125→            continue
   126→        status = str(entry.get("status", "")).strip()
   127→        summary = str(entry.get("summary", "")).strip()
   128→        note = str(entry.get("note", "")).strip()
   129→
   130→        line = f"  - [{status}] {summary}"
   131→        if note:
   132→            line += f" (note: {note})"
   133→        lines.append(line)
   134→    return "\n".join(lines) + "\n\n"
   135→
   136→
   137→def _concern_signal_lines(entry: dict[str, object]) -> list[str]:
   138→    """Render one concern signal entry into prompt lines."""
   139→    file = str(entry.get("file", "")).strip() or "(unknown file)"
   140→    concern_type = str(entry.get("type", "")).strip() or "design_concern"
   141→    summary = str(entry.get("summary", "")).strip()
   142→    question = str(entry.get("question", "")).strip()
   143→    evidence_raw = entry.get("evidence", [])
   144→    evidence = (
   145→        [str(item).strip() for item in evidence_raw if isinstance(item, str) and item.strip()]
   146→        if isinstance(evidence_raw, list)
   147→        else []
   148→    )
   149→    lines = [f"  - [{concern_type}] {file}"]
   150→    if summary:
   151→        lines.append(f"    summary: {summary}")
   152→    if question:
   153→        lines.append(f"    question: {question}")
   154→    lines.extend(f"    evidence: {snippet}" for snippet in evidence[:2])
   155→    return lines
   156→
   157→
   158→def _iter_valid_concern_signals(
   159→    signals: list[object],
   160→) -> list[dict[str, object]]:
   161→    """Filter signal entries to mapping payloads only."""
   162→    return [entry for entry in signals if isinstance(entry, dict)]
   163→
   164→
   165→def render_mechanical_concern_signals(batch: dict[str, object]) -> str:
   166→    """Render mechanically-generated concern hypotheses for this batch."""
   167→    signals = batch.get("concern_signals")
   168→    if not isinstance(signals, list) or not signals:
   169→        return ""
   170→
   171→    lines: list[str] = []
   172→    lines.append("Mechanical concern signals — navigation aid, not scoring evidence:")
   173→    lines.append(
   174→        "Confirm or refute each with your own code reading. Report only confirmed defects."
   175→    )
   176→
   177→    valid_signals = _iter_valid_concern_signals(signals)
   178→    capped_signals = valid_signals[:8]
   179→    for entry in capped_signals:
   180→        lines.extend(_concern_signal_lines(entry))
   181→
   182→    extra = max(0, len(valid_signals) - len(capped_signals))
   183→    if extra:
   184→        lines.append(f"  - (+{extra} more concern signals)")
   185→    return "\n".join(lines) + "\n\n"
   186→
   187→
   188→def render_workflow_integrity_focus(dim_set: set[str]) -> str:
   189→    """Render workflow integrity checks for architecture/integration dimensions."""
   190→    if not dim_set.intersection(
   191→        {
   192→            "cross_module_architecture",
   193→            "high_level_elegance",
   194→            "mid_level_elegance",
   195→            "design_coherence",
   196→            "initialization_coupling",
   197→        }
   198→    ):
   199→        return ""
   200→    return (
   201→        "9j. Workflow integrity checks: when reviewing orchestration/queue/review flows,\n"
   202→        "    explicitly look for loop-prone patterns and blind spots:\n"
   203→        "    - repeated stale/reopen churn without clear exit criteria or gating,\n"
   204→        "    - packet/batch data being generated but dropped before prompt execution,\n"
   205→        "    - ranking/triage logic that can starve target-improving work,\n"
   206→        "    - reruns happening before existing open review work is drained.\n"
   207→        "    If found, propose concrete guardrails and where to implement them.\n"
   208→    )
   209→
   210→
   211→def render_package_org_focus(dim_set: set[str]) -> str:
   212→    if "package_organization" not in dim_set:
   213→        return ""
   214→    return (
   215→        "9a. For package_organization, ground scoring in objective structure signals from "
   216→        "`holistic_context.structure` (root_files fan_in/fan_out roles, directory_profiles, "
   217→        "coupling_matrix). Prefer thresholded evidence (for example: fan_in < 5 for root "
   218→        "stragglers, import-affinity > 60%, directories > 10 files with mixed concerns).\n"
   219→        "9b. Suggestions must include a staged reorg plan (target folders, move order, "
   220→        "and import-update/validation commands).\n"
   221→        "9c. Also consult `holistic_context.structure.flat_dir_issues` for directories "
   222→        "flagged as overloaded, fragmented, or thin-wrapper patterns.\n"
   223→    )
   224→
   225→
   226→def render_abstraction_focus(dim_set: set[str]) -> str:
   227→    if "abstraction_fitness" not in dim_set:
   228→        return ""
   229→    return (
   230→        "9d. For abstraction_fitness, use evidence from `holistic_context.abstractions`:\n"
   231→        "  - `delegation_heavy_classes`: classes where most methods forward to an inner "
   232→        "object — entries include class_name, delegate_target, sample_methods, and line number.\n"
   233→        "  - `facade_modules`: re-export-only modules with high re_export_ratio — entries "
   234→        "include samples (re-exported names) and loc.\n"
   235→        "  - `typed_dict_violations`: TypedDict fields accessed via .get()/.setdefault()/.pop() "
   236→        "— entries include typed_dict_name, violation_type, field, and line number.\n"
   237→        "  - `complexity_hotspots`: files where mechanical analysis found extreme parameter "
   238→        "counts, deep nesting, or disconnected responsibility clusters.\n"
   239→        "  Include `delegation_density`, `definition_directness`, and `type_discipline` "
   240→        "alongside existing sub-axes in dimension_notes when evidence supports it.\n"
   241→    )
   242→
   243→
   244→def render_dimension_focus(dim_set: set[str]) -> str:
   245→    return (
   246→        render_package_org_focus(dim_set)
   247→        + render_abstraction_focus(dim_set)
   248→        + render_scan_evidence_focus(dim_set)
   249→        + render_workflow_integrity_focus(dim_set)
   250→    )
   251→
   252→
   253→def explode_to_single_dimension(
   254→    batches: list[dict[str, object]],
   255→    dimension_prompts: dict[str, dict[str, object]] | None = None,
   256→) -> list[dict[str, object]]:
   257→    """Split multi-dimension batches into one batch per dimension.
   258→
   259→    Preserves seed files and rationale — each exploded batch keeps the same
   260→    file grouping but is scoped to a single dimension.  When *dimension_prompts*
   261→    is provided, each exploded batch gets a ``_dimension_prompt`` key with the
   262→    prompt for its single dimension so that downstream renderers can use it
   263→    without extra parameter threading.
   264→    """
   265→    prompts = dimension_prompts or {}
   266→    result: list[dict[str, object]] = []
   267→    for batch in batches:
   268→        dims = batch.get("dimensions", [])
   269→        if not isinstance(dims, list):
   270→            result.append(batch)
   271→            continue
   272→        for dim in dims:
   273→            exploded: dict[str, object] = {**batch, "dimensions": [dim]}
   274→            dim_prompt = prompts.get(dim)
   275→            if isinstance(dim_prompt, dict):
   276→                exploded["_dimension_prompt"] = dim_prompt
   277→            result.append(exploded)
   278→    return result
   279→
   280→
   281→def render_dimension_prompts_block(
   282→    dimensions: tuple[str, ...],
   283→    dimension_prompts: dict[str, dict[str, object]],
   284→) -> str:
   285→    """Render inline dimension guidance so the reviewer sees the full rubric."""
   286→    if not dimensions or not dimension_prompts:
   287→        return ""
   288→    lines: list[str] = ["DIMENSION TO EVALUATE:\n"]
   289→    for dim in dimensions:
   290→        prompt = dimension_prompts.get(dim)
   291→        if not isinstance(prompt, dict):
   292→            lines.append(f"## {dim}\n(no rubric available)\n")
   293→            continue
   294→        description = str(prompt.get("description", "")).strip()
   295→        lines.append(f"## {dim}")
   296→        if description:
   297→            lines.append(description)
   298→
   299→        look_for = prompt.get("look_for")
   300→        if isinstance(look_for, list) and look_for:
   301→            lines.append("Look for:")
   302→            for item in look_for:
   303→                lines.append(f"- {item}")
   304→
   305→        skip = prompt.get("skip")
   306→        if isinstance(skip, list) and skip:
   307→            lines.append("Skip:")
   308→            for item in skip:
   309→                lines.append(f"- {item}")
   310→        lines.append("")
   311→    return "\n".join(lines) + "\n"
   312→
   313→
   314→def render_scoring_frame() -> str:
   315→    return (
   316→        "YOUR TASK: Read the code for this batch's dimension. Judge "
   317→        "how well the codebase serves a developer from that perspective. The dimension "
   318→        "rubric above defines what good looks like. "
   319→        "Cite specific observations that explain your judgment.\n\n"
   320→    )
   321→
   322→
   323→def render_scan_evidence_note() -> str:
   324→    return (
   325→        "Mechanical scan evidence — navigation aid, not scoring evidence:\n"
   326→        "The blind packet contains `holistic_context.scan_evidence` with aggregated signals "
   327→        "from all mechanical detectors — including complexity hotspots, error hotspots, signal "
   328→        "density index, boundary violations, and systemic patterns. Use these as starting "
   329→        "points for where to look beyond the seed files.\n\n"
   330→    )
   331→
   332→
   333→def render_seed_files_block(context: PromptBatchContext) -> str:
   334→    return f"Seed files (start here):\n{context.seed_files_text}\n\n"
   335→
   336→
   337→def render_task_requirements(*, issues_cap: int, dim_set: set[str]) -> str:
   338→    dim_focus = render_dimension_focus(dim_set)
   339→    # Build numbered items; dimension focus items get renumbered dynamically.
   340→    lines = [
   341→        "Task requirements:",
   342→        "1. Read the blind packet's `system_prompt` — it contains scoring rules and calibration.",
   343→        "2. Start from the seed files, then freely explore the repository to build your understanding.",
   344→        "3. Keep issues and scoring scoped to this batch's dimension.",
   345→        "4. Respect scope controls: do not include files/directories marked by `exclude`, `suppress`, or non-production zone overrides.",
   346→        f"5. Return 0-{issues_cap} issues for this batch (empty array allowed).",
   347→    ]
   348→    next_num = 6
   349→    if dim_focus:
   350→        for focus_line in dim_focus.rstrip("\n").split("\n"):
   351→            lines.append(f"{next_num}. {focus_line.lstrip('0123456789abcdefghij. ')}")
   352→            next_num += 1
   353→    lines.append(
   354→        f"{next_num}. Complete `dimension_judgment` for your dimension — all three fields "
   355→        "(strengths, issue_character, score_rationale) are required. Write the judgment BEFORE setting the score."
   356→    )
   357→    next_num += 1
   358→    lines.append(f"{next_num}. Do not edit repository files.")
   359→    next_num += 1
   360→    lines.append(f"{next_num}. Return ONLY valid JSON, no markdown fences.")
   361→    return "\n".join(lines) + "\n\n"
   362→
   363→
   364→def render_scope_enums() -> str:
   365→    return (
   366→        "Scope enums:\n"
   367→        '- impact_scope: "local" | "module" | "subsystem" | "codebase"\n'
   368→        '- fix_scope: "single_edit" | "multi_file_refactor" | "architectural_change"\n\n'
   369→    )
   370→
   371→
   372→def join_non_empty_sections(*sections: str) -> str:
   373→    return "".join(section for section in sections if section)
   374→
   375→
   376→__all__ = [
   377→    "PromptBatchContext",
   378→    "coerce_string_list",
   379→    "build_batch_context",
   380→    "explode_to_single_dimension",
   381→    "render_dimension_prompts_block",
   382→    "SCAN_EVIDENCE_FOCUS_BY_DIMENSION",
   383→    "render_scan_evidence_focus",
   384→    "render_historical_focus",
```

```
     5→from desloppify.base.discovery.file_paths import resolve_path

     9→
    10→def _abs(filepath: str) -> str:
    11→    """Resolve filepath to absolute using resolve_path."""
    12→    return resolve_path(filepath)
    13→
    14→
    15→def _read_file_contents(files: list[str]) -> dict[str, str]:
    16→    file_contents: dict[str, str] = {}
    17→    for filepath in files:
    18→        content = read_file_text(_abs(filepath))
    19→        if content is not None:
    20→            file_contents[filepath] = content
    21→    return file_contents
    22→
```

```
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import logging
     6→import re
     7→from collections import Counter
     8→from pathlib import Path
     9→from typing import Any
    10→
    11→from desloppify.base.discovery.file_paths import (
    12→
    13→    rel,
    14→
    15→    resolve_path,
    16→
    17→)
    18→from desloppify.base.output.fallbacks import log_best_effort_failure
    19→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import StateModel
    20→from desloppify.intelligence.review._context.patterns import (
    21→    ERROR_PATTERNS as _ERROR_PATTERNS,
    22→)
    23→from desloppify.intelligence.review._context.patterns import (
    24→    FUNC_NAME_RE as _FUNC_NAME_RE,
    25→)
    26→from desloppify.intelligence.review._context.patterns import (
    27→    extract_imported_names as _extract_imported_names,
    28→)
    29→from desloppify.intelligence.review.context import file_excerpt, importer_count
    30→
    31→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    32→
    33→
    34→def architecture_context(lang: object, file_contents: dict[str, str]) -> dict[str, Any]:
    35→    arch: dict[str, Any] = {}
    36→    if not lang.dep_graph:
    37→        return arch
    38→
    39→    importer_counts = {}
    40→    for filepath, entry in lang.dep_graph.items():
    41→        entry_importer_count = importer_count(entry)
    42→        if entry_importer_count > 0:
    43→            importer_counts[rel(filepath)] = entry_importer_count
    44→    top_imported = sorted(importer_counts.items(), key=lambda item: -item[1])[:10]
    45→    arch["god_modules"] = [
    46→        {"file": filepath, "importers": count, "excerpt": file_excerpt(filepath) or ""}
    47→        for filepath, count in top_imported
    48→        if count >= 5
    49→    ]
    50→    arch["top_imported"] = dict(top_imported)
    51→    return arch
    52→
    53→
    54→def coupling_context(file_contents: dict[str, str]) -> dict[str, Any]:
    55→    coupling: dict[str, Any] = {}
    56→    module_level_io = []
    57→    for filepath, content in file_contents.items():
    58→        for idx, raw_line in enumerate(content.splitlines()[:50]):
    59→            stripped = raw_line.strip()
    60→            if stripped.startswith(
    61→                ("def ", "class ", "async def ", "if ", "#", "@", "import ", "from ")
    62→            ):
    63→                continue
    64→            if re.search(
    65→                r"\b(?:open|connect|requests?\.|urllib|subprocess|os\.system)\b",
    66→                stripped,
    67→            ):
    68→                module_level_io.append(
    69→                    {
    70→                        "file": rel(filepath),
    71→                        "line": idx + 1,
    72→                        "code": stripped[:100],
    73→                    }
    74→                )
    75→    if module_level_io:
    76→        coupling["module_level_io"] = module_level_io[:20]
    77→    return coupling
    78→
    79→
    80→def naming_conventions_context(file_contents: dict[str, str]) -> dict[str, Any]:
    81→    dir_styles: dict[str, Counter] = {}
    82→    for filepath, content in file_contents.items():
    83→        parts = Path(filepath).parts
    84→        if len(parts) < 2:
    85→            continue
    86→        dir_name = parts[-2] + "/"
    87→        counter = dir_styles.setdefault(dir_name, Counter())
    88→        for name in _FUNC_NAME_RE.findall(content):
    89→            if "_" in name and name.islower():
    90→                counter["snake_case"] += 1
    91→            elif name[0].islower() and any(ch.isupper() for ch in name):
    92→                counter["camelCase"] += 1
    93→            elif name[0].isupper():
    94→                counter["PascalCase"] += 1
    95→    return {
    96→        name: dict(counter.most_common(3))
    97→        for name, counter in dir_styles.items()
    98→        if sum(counter.values()) >= 3
    99→    }
   100→
```
