    44→def build_holistic_context(
    45→    path: Path,
    46→    lang: object,
    47→    state: dict,
    48→    files: list[str] | None = None,
    49→) -> dict[str, object]:
    50→    """Gather codebase-wide data for holistic review."""
    51→    return build_holistic_context_model(path, lang, state, files=files).to_dict()
    52→
    53→
    54→def build_holistic_context_model(
    55→    path: Path,
    56→    lang: object,
    57→    state: dict,
    58→    files: list[str] | None = None,
    59→) -> HolisticContext:
    60→    """Gather holistic context and return a typed context contract."""
    61→    selected_files = select_holistic_files(path, lang, files)
    62→
    63→    already_cached = is_file_cache_enabled()
    64→    if not already_cached:
    65→        enable_file_cache()
    66→    try:
    67→        return _build_holistic_context_inner(path, selected_files, lang, state)
    68→    finally:
    69→        if not already_cached:
    70→            disable_file_cache()
    71→
    72→
    73→def _build_holistic_context_inner(
    74→    path: Path, files: list[str], lang: object, state: dict
    75→) -> HolisticContext:
    76→    """Inner holistic context builder (runs with file cache enabled)."""
    77→    file_contents = _read_file_contents(files)
    78→    allowed_rel_files = {
    79→        rel(filepath)
    80→        for filepath in files
    81→        if isinstance(filepath, str) and filepath
    82→    }
    83→
    84→    context = HolisticContext(
    85→        architecture=_architecture_context(lang, file_contents),
    86→        coupling=_coupling_context(file_contents),
    87→        conventions={
    88→            "naming_by_directory": _naming_conventions_context(file_contents),
    89→            "sibling_behavior": _sibling_behavior_context(file_contents, base_path=path),
    90→        },
    91→        errors={
    92→            "strategy_by_directory": _error_strategy_context(file_contents),
    93→        },
    94→        abstractions=_abstractions_context(file_contents),
    95→        dependencies=_dependencies_context(state, allowed_files=allowed_rel_files),
    96→        testing=_testing_context(
    97→            lang,
    98→            state,
    99→            file_contents,
   100→            allowed_files=allowed_rel_files,
   101→        ),
   102→        api_surface=_api_surface_context(lang, file_contents),
   103→        structure=compute_structure_context(file_contents, lang),
   104→    )
   105→
   106→    auth_ctx = gather_auth_context(file_contents, rel_fn=rel)
   107→    if auth_ctx:
   108→        context.authorization = auth_ctx
   109→
   110→    ai_debt = gather_ai_debt_signals(file_contents, rel_fn=rel)
   111→    if ai_debt.get("file_signals"):
   112→        context.ai_debt_signals = ai_debt
   113→
   114→    migration = gather_migration_signals(file_contents, lang, rel_fn=rel)
   115→    if migration:
   116→        context.migration_signals = migration
   117→
   118→    context.codebase_stats = _codebase_stats(file_contents)
   119→
   120→    # Enrich with aggregated mechanical detector evidence.
   121→    evidence = gather_mechanical_evidence(state, allowed_files=allowed_rel_files)
   122→    if evidence:
   123→        context.scan_evidence = evidence
   124→        _enrich_sections_from_evidence(context, evidence)
   125→
   126→    context.normalize_sections(strict=True)
   127→    return context
   128→
   129→
   130→def _enrich_sections_from_evidence(
   131→    context: HolisticContext, evidence: dict
   132→) -> None:
   133→    """Merge mechanical evidence into existing holistic context sections."""
   134→    if "complexity_hotspots" in evidence:
   135→        context.abstractions["complexity_hotspots"] = evidence["complexity_hotspots"]
   136→    if "error_hotspots" in evidence:
   137→        context.errors["exception_hotspots"] = evidence["error_hotspots"]
   138→    if "mutable_globals" in evidence:
   139→        context.errors["mutable_globals"] = evidence["mutable_globals"]
   140→    if "boundary_violations" in evidence:
   141→        context.coupling["boundary_violations"] = evidence["boundary_violations"]
   142→    if "deferred_import_density" in evidence:
   143→        context.dependencies["deferred_import_density"] = evidence["deferred_import_density"]
   144→    if "duplicate_clusters" in evidence:
   145→        context.conventions["duplicate_clusters"] = evidence["duplicate_clusters"]
   146→    if "naming_drift" in evidence:
   147→        context.conventions["naming_drift"] = evidence["naming_drift"]
   148→    if "flat_dir_issues" in evidence:
   149→        context.structure["flat_dir_issues"] = evidence["flat_dir_issues"]

1→"""Aggregate mechanical detector issues into structured evidence clusters.
     2→
     3→Reads all state issues and produces signal clusters organized for holistic
     4→review context enrichment.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→from __future__ import annotations
     8→
     9→from collections import Counter, defaultdict
    10→from typing import Any
    11→
    12→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import StateModel
    13→
    14→from ._clusters_complexity import _build_complexity_hotspots
    15→from ._clusters_consistency import _build_duplicate_clusters, _build_naming_drift
    16→from ._clusters_dependency import (
    17→    _build_boundary_violations,
    18→    _build_dead_code,
    19→    _build_deferred_import_density,
    20→    _build_private_crossings,
    21→)
    22→from ._clusters_error_state import _build_error_hotspots, _build_mutable_globals
    23→from ._clusters_organization import (
    24→    _build_flat_dir_issues,
    25→    _build_large_file_distribution,
    26→)
    27→from ._clusters_security import (
    28→    _build_security_hotspots,
    29→    _build_signal_density,
    30→    _build_systemic_patterns,
    31→)
    32→
    33→
    34→def _normalize_allowed_files(
    35→    allowed_files: set[str] | list[str] | tuple[str, ...] | None,
    36→) -> set[str] | None:
    37→    """Normalize optional allowed-file scope to slash-normalized relative paths."""
    38→    if allowed_files is None:
    39→        return None
    40→    out: set[str] = set()
    41→    for raw in allowed_files:
    42→        if not isinstance(raw, str):
    43→            continue
    44→        file_path = raw.strip().replace("\\", "/")
    45→        if file_path:
    46→            out.add(file_path)
    47→    return out
    48→
    49→
    50→def gather_mechanical_evidence(
    51→    state: StateModel,
    52→    *,
    53→    allowed_files: set[str] | list[str] | tuple[str, ...] | None = None,
    54→) -> dict[str, Any]:
    55→    """Aggregate open issues into evidence clusters for holistic review."""
    56→    issues = state.get("issues", {})
    57→    if not issues:
    58→        return {}
    59→    allowed_scope = _normalize_allowed_files(allowed_files)
    60→
    61→    by_detector: dict[str, list[dict]] = defaultdict(list)
    62→    by_file: dict[str, list[dict]] = defaultdict(list)
    63→    smell_counter: Counter[str] = Counter()
    64→    smell_files: dict[str, list[str]] = defaultdict(list)
    65→
    66→    for issue in issues.values():
    67→        if not isinstance(issue, dict):
    68→            continue
    69→        if issue.get("status") != "open":
    70→            continue
    71→        filepath = issue.get("file", "")
    72→        normalized_file = filepath.strip().replace("\\", "/") if isinstance(filepath, str) else ""
    73→        if allowed_scope is not None and normalized_file not in allowed_scope:
    74→            continue
    75→        det = issue.get("detector", "")
    76→        if det:
    77→            by_detector[det].append(issue)
    78→        if normalized_file and normalized_file != ".":
    79→            by_file[normalized_file].append(issue)
    80→        if det == "smells":
    81→            detail = issue.get("detail", {})
    82→            smell_id = detail.get("smell_id", "") if isinstance(detail, dict) else ""
    83→            if smell_id:
    84→                smell_counter[smell_id] += 1
    85→                smell_files[smell_id].append(normalized_file)
    86→
    87→    if not by_detector:
    88→        return {}
    89→
    90→    evidence: dict[str, Any] = {}
    91→
    92→    complexity_hotspots = _build_complexity_hotspots(by_detector, by_file)
    93→    if complexity_hotspots:
    94→        evidence["complexity_hotspots"] = complexity_hotspots
    95→
    96→    error_hotspots = _build_error_hotspots(by_detector)
    97→    if error_hotspots:
    98→        evidence["error_hotspots"] = error_hotspots
    99→
   100→    mutable_globals = _build_mutable_globals(by_detector)
   101→    if mutable_globals:
   102→        evidence["mutable_globals"] = mutable_globals
   103→
   104→    boundary_violations = _build_boundary_violations(by_detector)
   105→    if boundary_violations:
   106→        evidence["boundary_violations"] = boundary_violations
   107→
   108→    dead_code = _build_dead_code(by_detector)
   109→    if dead_code:
   110→        evidence["dead_code"] = dead_code
   111→
   112→    private_crossings = _build_private_crossings(by_detector)
   113→    if private_crossings:
   114→        evidence["private_crossings"] = private_crossings
   115→
   116→    deferred = _build_deferred_import_density(by_file)
   117→    if deferred:
   118→        evidence["deferred_import_density"] = deferred
   119→
   120→    duplicate_clusters = _build_duplicate_clusters(by_detector)
   121→    if duplicate_clusters:
   122→        evidence["duplicate_clusters"] = duplicate_clusters
   123→
   124→    naming_drift = _build_naming_drift(by_detector)
   125→    if naming_drift:
   126→        evidence["naming_drift"] = naming_drift
   127→
   128→    flat_dir_issues = _build_flat_dir_issues(by_detector)
   129→    if flat_dir_issues:
   130→        evidence["flat_dir_issues"] = flat_dir_issues
   131→
   132→    large_dist = _build_large_file_distribution(by_detector)
   133→    if large_dist:
   134→        evidence["large_file_distribution"] = large_dist
   135→
   136→    security_hotspots = _build_security_hotspots(by_detector)
   137→    if security_hotspots:
   138→        evidence["security_hotspots"] = security_hotspots
   139→
   140→    signal_density = _build_signal_density(by_file)
   141→    if signal_density:
   142→        evidence["signal_density"] = signal_density
   143→
   144→    systemic = _build_systemic_patterns(smell_counter, smell_files)
   145→    if systemic:
   146→        evidence["systemic_patterns"] = systemic
   147→
   148→    pkg_census = _build_package_size_census(by_file)
   149→    if pkg_census:
   150→        evidence["package_size_census"] = pkg_census
   151→
   152→    return evidence
   153→
   154→
   155→def _build_package_size_census(
   156→    by_file: dict[str, list[dict]],
   157→) -> list[dict]:
   158→    """Compute LOC per top-level package.  Flag packages >15% of codebase."""
   159→    pkg_loc: dict[str, int] = {}
   160→    for filepath, issues in by_file.items():
   161→        parts = filepath.replace("\\", "/").split("/")
   162→        pkg = parts[0] if parts else filepath
   163→        # Sum file LOC from structural detail or count issues as proxy.
   164→        file_loc = 0
   165→        for issue in issues:
   166→            if issue.get("detector") == "structural":
   167→                detail = issue.get("detail", {})
   168→                if isinstance(detail, dict):
   169→                    loc_val = detail.get("loc", 0)
   170→                    if isinstance(loc_val, int | float) and loc_val > file_loc:
   171→                        file_loc = int(loc_val)
   172→        if file_loc == 0:
   173→            file_loc = 1  # Count the file at minimum
   174→        pkg_loc[pkg] = pkg_loc.get(pkg, 0) + file_loc
   175→
   176→    total_loc = sum(pkg_loc.values())
   177→    if total_loc == 0:
   178→        return []
   179→
   180→    results = []
   181→    for pkg, loc in sorted(pkg_loc.items(), key=lambda kv: -kv[1]):
   182→        pct = round(100 * loc / total_loc, 1)
   183→        results.append({
   184→            "package": pkg,
   185→            "loc": loc,
   186→            "pct_of_total": pct,
   187→            "disproportionate": pct > 15,
   188→        })
   189→    return results
   190→
   191→
   192→__all__ = ["gather_mechanical_evidence"]
   193→
```

    32→    lang: Any,

    35→    """Append an optional cross-cutting full-codebase batch."""

    39→    for filepath in all_files:

    41→            zone = lang.zone_map.get(filepath)
    42→            if zone_in(zone, REVIEW_SELECTION_EXCLUDED_ZONES):
    43→                continue
    44→        all_rel_files.append(rel(filepath))
    45→        if isinstance(max_files, int) and max_files > 0 and len(all_rel_files) >= max_files:
    46→            break
    47→    if not all_rel_files:
    48→        return
    49→    batches.append(
    50→        {
    51→            "name": "Full Codebase Sweep",
    52→            "dimensions": list(dims),
    53→            "files_to_read": all_rel_files,
    54→            "why": "thorough default: evaluate cross-cutting quality across all production files",
    55→        }
    56→    )

1→"""Data models for review-context construction."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from collections.abc import Mapping
     6→from dataclasses import dataclass, field
     7→from typing import Any, TypeAlias
     8→
     9→
    10→class ReviewContextSchemaError(ValueError):
    11→    """Raised when contextual review payloads violate section-shape contracts."""
    12→
    13→
    14→SectionPayload: TypeAlias = dict[str, Any]
    15→
    16→
    17→def _empty_section(name: str) -> SectionPayload:
    18→    _ = name
    19→    return {}
    20→
    21→
    22→def _coerce_section(
    23→    *,
    24→    section: str,
    25→    value: object,
    26→    strict: bool,
    27→) -> SectionPayload:
    28→    if isinstance(value, Mapping):
    29→        return dict(value)
    30→    if value is None:
    31→        return {}
    32→    if strict:
    33→        raise ReviewContextSchemaError(
    34→            f"review context section '{section}' must be an object, got {type(value).__name__}"
    35→        )
    36→    return {}
    37→
    38→
    39→@dataclass
    40→class ReviewContext:
    41→    """Codebase-wide context for contextual file evaluation."""
    42→
    43→    naming_vocabulary: SectionPayload = field(
    44→        default_factory=lambda: _empty_section("naming_vocabulary")
    45→    )
    46→    error_conventions: SectionPayload = field(
    47→        default_factory=lambda: _empty_section("error_conventions")
    48→    )
    49→    module_patterns: SectionPayload = field(
    50→        default_factory=lambda: _empty_section("module_patterns")
    51→    )
    52→    import_graph_summary: SectionPayload = field(
    53→        default_factory=lambda: _empty_section("import_graph_summary")
    54→    )
    55→    zone_distribution: SectionPayload = field(
    56→        default_factory=lambda: _empty_section("zone_distribution")
    57→    )
    58→    existing_issues: SectionPayload = field(
    59→        default_factory=lambda: _empty_section("existing_issues")
    60→    )
    61→    codebase_stats: SectionPayload = field(
    62→        default_factory=lambda: _empty_section("codebase_stats")
    63→    )
    64→    sibling_conventions: SectionPayload = field(
    65→        default_factory=lambda: _empty_section("sibling_conventions")
    66→    )
    67→    ai_debt_signals: SectionPayload = field(
    68→        default_factory=lambda: _empty_section("ai_debt_signals")
    69→    )
    70→    auth_patterns: SectionPayload = field(
    71→        default_factory=lambda: _empty_section("auth_patterns")
    72→    )
    73→    error_strategies: SectionPayload = field(
    74→        default_factory=lambda: _empty_section("error_strategies")
    75→    )
    76→
    77→    _SECTION_NAMES = (
    78→        "naming_vocabulary",
    79→        "error_conventions",
    80→        "module_patterns",
    81→        "import_graph_summary",
    82→        "zone_distribution",
    83→        "existing_issues",
    84→        "codebase_stats",
    85→        "sibling_conventions",
    86→        "ai_debt_signals",
    87→        "auth_patterns",
    88→        "error_strategies",
    89→    )
    90→
    91→    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
    92→        self.normalize_sections(strict=True)
    93→
    94→    def normalize_sections(self, *, strict: bool) -> None:
    95→        for section in self._SECTION_NAMES:
    96→            setattr(
    97→                self,
    98→                section,
    99→                _coerce_section(section=section, value=getattr(self, section), strict=strict),
   100→            )

95→def render_historical_focus(batch: dict[str, object]) -> str:

    97→    if not isinstance(focus, dict):

    99→

   101→    try:

   114→    lines.append(

   119→        " issues that have been fixed or marked wontfix — focus on what remains or"

   124→        if not isinstance(entry, dict):
   125→            continue
   126→        status = str(entry.get("status", "")).strip()
   127→        summary = str(entry.get("summary", "")).strip()
   128→        note = str(entry.get("note", "")).strip()
   129→
   130→        line = f"  - [{status}] {summary}"
   131→        if note:
   132→            line += f" (note: {note})"
   133→        lines.append(line)
   134→    return "\n".join(lines) + "\n\n"
```

        return {}

Perfect! Now I have enough information. Let me create a comprehensive summary of my findings:

## Summary: How Wontfix Issues Are Presented to the LLM Reviewer During Holistic Review

I've thoroughly searched the codebase and found clear answers to all four key questions. Here's what I discovered:

### **1. Does the reviewer see wontfixed issues at all?**

**YES, but with significant filtering:**

- **In mechanical holistic context**: Wontfix issues are **COMPLETELY EXCLUDED**. In `desloppify/intelligence/review/context_holistic/mechanical.py:69`, there's a hard filter:
  ```python
  if issue.get("status") != "open":
      continue
  ```
  This means wontfix issues never appear in the `scan_evidence` sections (complexity_hotspots, error_hotspots, boundary_violations, etc.).

- **In per-file existing issues context**: Wontfix issues are **COMPLETELY EXCLUDED**. In `desloppify/intelligence/review/context_builder.py:103`, the same filter applies:
  ```python
  if issue.get("status") != "open":
      continue
  ```
  This populates the `existing_issues` dict shown to reviewers.

- **In historical issue context**: Wontfix issues **ARE INCLUDED**. They appear in the historical review issues list built by `desloppify/intelligence/review/_prepare/issue_history.py:116-175`, specifically in `build_issue_history_context()`. This includes issues with status "open", "fixed", "wontfix", "auto_resolved", etc.

### **2. If yes, does it see the wontfix reason/note?**

**YES, for historical issues only.** The wontfix note is preserved and displayed:
- In `_shape_issue()` (line 100-113 of issue_history.py), the note is extracted via `_meaningful_note()` 
- In `render_historical_focus()` (line 95-134 of prompt_sections.py), each historical issue is rendered as:
  ```
  - [status] summary (note: reason_if_present)
  ```

For example, a wontfix issue in the historical section would appear as:
```
- [wontfix] Legacy compatibility surface remains primary. (note: blocked by migration dependency)
```

### **3. Does the reviewer know it should treat wontfix differently from open?**

**YES, explicitly told via prompt guidance** (lines 115-121 of `prompt_sections.py`):

```
Previously flagged issues — navigation aid, not scoring evidence:
Check whether each issue still exists in the current code. Do not re-report
issues that have been fixed or marked wontfix — focus on what remains or
what is new. If several past issues share a root cause, call that out.
```

This guidance is explicit in the batch prompt that:
1. Previously flagged issues are "navigation aid, not scoring evidence"
2. Wontfix issues should NOT be re-reported
3. The reviewer should focus on what remains or what is new

### **4. Is there any prompt text that tells the reviewer how to handle wontfix items?**

**YES.** The key guidance is in `render_historical_focus()` output which:
- Lists all historical review issues with their status visible: `[open]`, `[fixed]`, `[wontfix]`, etc.
- Includes the wontfix reason/note if one exists
- Instructs the reviewer not to re-report wontfix items
- Tells the reviewer to focus on NEW issues or issues where the wontfix has become invalid

### **Key Architecture Insight**

The design implements a **two-tier approach**:

1. **Mechanical evidence (scan_evidence)**: Strictly filtered to OPEN issues only. Wontfix doesn't appear in hotspots, signal density, or other mechanical clusters.

2. **Historical context**: Includes ALL resolution statuses (open/fixed/wontfix/auto_resolved) with explicit instructions that wontfix should not be re-reported.

This creates an asymmetry: the reviewer sees wontfix historical issues as **reference context** but is explicitly told to treat them differently from open issues. The mechanical evidence never includes wontfix, ensuring the analysis is fresh and focuses on current open problems.

- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/context_builder.py` - Filters to open only (line 103)
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/_prepare/issue_history.py` - Includes all statuses with note extraction (lines 100-113, 116-175)
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/prompt_sections.py` - Renders historical context with status and note (lines 95-134)
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/tests/review/context/test_issue_history_context.py` - Tests showing wontfix with notes (lines 70-111)
